Raghav, Mr. Vimal Kumar Jatav, Mr.
Mahendra Shandilya, Mr. Om Parkash
Sharma, Mr. Khurshid Ahmed Khan,
Mr. Rahul Tiwari, Mr.Ashindra Gautam,
Mr. Y.K. Sharma, Mr. Sanjay Khedar,
Ms. Vandana Sharma, Mr. Y.S. Jadoun,
Mr. Mohit Khandelwal, Mr. Vijay
Pathak, Ms.Sunita Sharma, Mr. D.S.
Dhariwal, Mr.Indresh Sharma, Mr.
Suresh Kumar Dhenwal, Mr. Rajendra
Sharma, Mr.Amit Jindal, Mr. Banwari
Lal Saini, Mr. Santosh Kumar Soni, Mr.
Kunal Jaiman, Ms. Aradhana Swami,
Mr.Gaurav Jain, Mr. Rajpal Dhankhar &
Mr. Shree Ram Dhakar,
For Respondent(s) : Ms. Sheetal Mirdha, AAG with
Mr. Prateek Singh
Mr. Sandeep Pathak with Mr. Noor
Ahmad
Mr. Ribhu Dutt
Mr. R.A. Katta
Mr. Arnav Singh
HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE SANJEEV PRAKASH SHARMA
Judgment / Order
08/07/2020

(175 of 179) [CW-5798/2020]
Ms. Sheetal Mirdha, AAG and Mr. RA Katta, Advocate accept
notices on behalf of the respondents in all the writ petitions.
All these writ petitions raise common prayers and are
commonly aggrieved of a decision taken by the respondents
whereby they laid down a Policy-2020 for starting Diploma in
Veterinary Live Stock Assistant in private sector for 2020.
It has been informed by AAG that a bunch of writ petitions
led by SBCWP No.12715/2019 Nagfani Institute of Animal
Husbandry Vs. State of Rajasthan Principal Seat at Jodhpur
directed the State to frame Policy in relation to
enhancement/regulation of the number of seats for 2 years
Diploma Course in Animal Husbandry. The policy was directed to
be framed within a period of four weeks and be placed before
2.1.2020. In pursuance of the directions aforementioned, a policy
was prepared and placed before the concerned court wherein
annual calendar for Live Stock Diploma Course was also laid down.
As per the said annual calendar, the last date for submission of
application for grant of NOC to the private institutions was fixed as
31.1.2020. It was also laid down in the policy that those
institutions which are already functioning in the said field for five
years or more would also be required to move an appropriate
application for seeking fresh NOC. The said application will have to
be moved within three months. Those institutions who had
received NOC earlier but have not started functioning for last two
years, will have to apply afresh upon the State Government asking
for such application by advertisement otherwise their earlier NOC
would also stand cancelled.
Learned AAG further submits that no advertisement has been
issued for the purpose of inviting applications from the institutions

(176 of 179) [CW-5798/2020]
for granting NOC. Learned AAG has also pointed out that earlier
vide order dt. 11.2.2004 in DB CWP No.5908/2003, this Court had
directed for constitution of a High Power Committee to examine
the issue with regard to the requirement of starting the course of
Live Stock Training Diploma. Learned counsel has submitted that
the said High Power Committee had observed as under:-
“(e) An enquiry should be conducted by
whomsoever the Government consider
appropriate to inquire into:-
(i) the reasons for the non-transparent method
adopted in inviting applications for opening
institutions in the private sector and fixing the
responsibility for the same.
(ii) If any shortcoming is found in the facilities
available in the institutes (in the inspections to be
conducted by the team led by the Collector of the
District) to fix responsibility of the officers who
gave erroneous or wrong reports earlier.
(iii) the reasons for the Department of Animal
Husbandry having carried out select inspections
instead of maintaining a date-wise priority list, so
that the inspections could have been made on a
‘first come first serve’ basis.
(f) the entire matter be placed before the Cabinet
for reconsideration, bringing to the notice of the
Cabinet all the relevant factors such as provisions
of the Indian Veterinary Council Act, the absence
of any recognition of this particular training
outside the State, the limited opportunities
available in the Government, the lack of trained
personnel for veterinary services vis-a-vis the
norm set by the ICAR, etc. Permission to new
institutions, or continuation of existing
institutions, would depend on the decision of the
Cabinet. Therefore, the permitted institutes
should be clearly and unambiguously informed
that they should not admit any new student for a
new batch till the final decision in the matter is
communicated to them. In case the Government
decides not to continue these institutes, then the
fee of Rs.2 lakhs given by these institutes should
be proportionately refunded to them.”

(177 of 179) [CW-5798/2020]
Learned counsel submits that while the aforesaid Policy 2020
has been rectified by the Chief Minister but in view of the High
Power Committee’s recommendations, as noted above, the matter
shall have to be placed before the Cabinet for rectification and to
examine the necessity of allowing the said courses keeping in view
the requirement of such courses in the State.
Counsel appearing for the petitioners submit that while the
aforesaid requirements may be done at the level of the State
Government, the petitioners cannot be deprived of submitting
their applications for consideration for grant of NOC.
It is submitted that while the policy was placed before the
concerned Principal Seat at Jodhpur, the same was not duly
published and in terms of the annual calendar laid down under the
policy, the last date i.e. 31.1.2020 has expired and the petitioners
could not move appropriate applications in terms of the calendar
laid down by the respondents and have therefore approached this
Court with the prayer that they may be allowed to move their
applications off-line for consideration for grant of NOC.
It is noticed that writ applications were filed by the various
institutions at Principal Seat, Jodhpur as well as this Bench and by
the interim orders, the institutes were directed to move their
applications for grant of NOC by a particular date. While this Court
had granted the writ petitioners to submit their applications latest
by 15th June, 2020, it is noticed that at Principal Seat, Jodhpur, a
Coordinate Bench has granted the last date of submitting the
applications as 15th July, 2020 and in one case i.e. SBCWP
No.4056/2020: Marudhara Medical And Education Society Versus
State of Rajasthan & Ors., date has been granted upto 20th July,

(178 of 179) [CW-5798/2020]
2020. Following it, in few cases this court also allowed petitioners
to file applications by 20th July, 2020.
Keeping in view thereof, this Court is of the firm view that
the State Government has exclusive domain to take a decision of
starting Diploma Course or as to whether it is viable for the
purpose of the State for giving employment. However institutes
which intend to start the courses or who have been providing such
training earlier, have a right of consideration for grant to NOC or
renewal and they cannot be simply ousted on the ground that the
calendar laid down applications to be filed upto 31st January, 2020
because the said calendar was not duly published.
 This Court notices that the calendar which has been
published in the policy, as otherwise has not been adhered to the
State and if the State actually needs to start the Diploma Courses,
it should prepare a fresh calendar for the said purpose. It is also
seen that the policy and viability of course is to examined by the
cabinet.
Be that as it may, since this Court has already allowed the
applications to be moved for grant of NOC and interim orders have
been passed by this Bench as well as at Principal Seat. Therefore,
instead of asking each and every institutes to approach this Court,
the State Government is directed to allow all the institutes, which
are intending to start the said Courses for applying NOC by issuing
a general advertisement and cut off date may be laid down
therein. Such advertisement would also mention other conditions
which the State Government may lay down for the purpose of
grant of NOC as well as for starting of the Courses. The conditions
of the policy may also be mentioned, if so required. Those
petitioners, who have already moved the applications, need not

(179 of 179) [CW-5798/2020]
file fresh applications for the said purpose. Those petitioners, who
are before this Court and in those cases where there is no interim
order, shall also be allowed to submit their applications by 20th
July, 2020.
Learned counsel appearing for the petitioners submit that if a
decision is taken by the State Government not to further proceed
for grant of NOC, then the fees which they have already deposited
alongwith their applications may be refunded to them. In this
regard, the Additional Advocate General submits that there should
be no difficulty if a decision is taken not to proceed with the
applications. It is further made clear that if any institute is
aggrieved of decision to refuse NOC or renewal, it shall be free to
approach this court afresh.
With the aforesaid observations, all these writ petitions stand
disposed of. All pending applications also stand disposed of.
(SANJEEV PRAKASH SHARMA),J
Brijesh 26-401
